V_{t,t_{1}}
.
For each
u\in V_{t,t_{2}}
, since

\mathcal{G}\models\left({\#_{x}\left[E(u,x)\land P_{t}(x)\land U_{t_{1}}(x)\right]}={\#_{x}\left[P_{t}(x)\land U_{t_{3}}(x)\right]}\right),

there exists
\left|V_{t,t_{3}}\right|
edges from
u
to the set
V_{t,t_{1}}
.
On the other hand,
for each
u\in V_{t,t_{1}}
,
since
\mathcal{G}\models\left({\#_{x}\left[E(x,u)\land P_{t}(x)\land U_{t_{2}}(x)\right]}=1\right)
,
there exists only one edge from the set
V_{t,t_{2}}
to
u
.
Therefore we have
1\cdot\left|V_{t,t_{1}}\right|=\left|V_{t,t_{3}}\right|\cdot\left|V_{t,t_{2}}\right|
.

Since the solvability (over
\mathbb{N}
) of simple equation systems is undecidable
[
16
]
, Theorem
4.23

follows. From the theorem and Corollary
4.22
we obtain undecidability of static analysis for GNNs with global readout:

Theorem 4.27
.

The satisfiability problem of
\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s is undecidable.

Using the same reduction, we also obtain undecidability for universal satisfiability:

Theorem 4.28
.

The universal satisfiability problem of
\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s is undecidable.

5
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-completeness of satisfiability for GNNs with local aggregation and
\mathsf{TrReLU}
activations

A more realistic analysis of complexity requires stronger assumptions on the activation functions.
For now we consider only one special case, where the activation function is truncated
\mathsf{ReLU}
:

Theorem 5.1
.

The satisfiability problem of
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s is
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-complete, and so is
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
.
They are
\mathsf{NP}
-complete when the number of layers is fixed.

We start with the
\mathsf{PSPACE}
upper bound argument. We can show that for an arbitrary input graph, there are only exponentially many activation values, each representable with a polynomial number of bits. We also show, via an “unravelling construction”, a common technique used in analysis of modal and guarded logics
[
14
,
9
]
, that a satisfying model
can be taken to be a tree of polynomial depth and branching. These two facts immediately give an elementary bound, since we could guess the tree and the activation values. We can improve to
\mathsf{PSPACE}
by exploring a satisfying tree-like model on-the-fly: again, this is in line with the
\mathsf{PSPACE}
algorithm for modal logic
[
14
]
.

The
\mathsf{PSPACE}
lower bound is established by embedding the description logic
\mathcal{ALC}
into
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
.

\mathsf{PSPACE}
-hardness will follow from this, since concept satisfiability problem of
\mathcal{ALC}
with one role is
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-hard
[
21
]
. The
\mathsf{NP}
upper bound will use the same on-the-fly algorithm as in the
\mathsf{PSPACE}
case, just observing that
for fixed number of layers it can be implemented in
\mathsf{NP}
. A direct encoding of
{3\text{-}\mathsf{SAT}}
gives the lower bound.

5.1
Exponential history-space property

The first step is to establish a bound on the size of the numbers that can be computed by GNNs. Remember that we begin with a graph where the feature values are only binary, and in each layer we do one aggregation and truncate the result. Thus it is intuitive that we cannot build up large values in any intermediate result at any node, regardless of the size of the graphs. This property actually holds even with global aggregation, but we prove it here only for the local case, since this is the only one relevant to this proof.
We first extend the idea of the spectrum, which is defined in Section
4
.

Definition 5.2
.

For an
n
-GNN
\mathcal{A}
,
for an
n
-graph
\mathcal{G}
, vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}
, and
0\leq\ell\leq L
,
the
\ell
-history of
v
in
\mathcal{G}
(w.r.t.
\mathcal{A}
),
denoted by

\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
,
is the tuple that collects the first
(\ell+1)
feature vectors of
v
.
Formally, for
0\leq t\leq\ell
,
\left(\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)\right)[t]=\xi^{\left(t\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
.

When the graph
\mathcal{G}
is clear from the context, we omit it
and simply write
\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}(v)
.

Definition 5.3
.

For an
n
-GNN
\mathcal{A}
and
0\leq\ell\leq L
,
the
\ell
-history-space
of
\mathcal{A}
,
denoted by
{{\mathcal{H}}^{\left(\ell\right)}}
,
is the set
\left\{\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)\middle|\ \text{for every $n$-graph $\mathcal{G}$ and vertex $v$ in $\mathcal{G}$}\right\}
.

For a vertex
v
,
the
\ell
-feacture of
v
is computed from
(\ell-1)
-features of vertices in the graph.
Similarly
\ell
-history of
v
can be computed efficiently from
(\ell-1)
-features.

Lemma 5.4
.

For an
n
-GNN
\mathcal{A}
,
for an
n
-graph
\mathcal{G}
, vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}
, and
0\leq\ell\leq L
,
the
\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}(v)
can be computed efficiently from
\xi^{\left(0\right)}(v)
and
\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell-1\right)}(u)
for vertices
u
in
\mathcal{G}
.

Proof 5.5
.

For
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}
,
let
\mathbf{h}_{x}=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{x}(v)}\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell-1\right)}(u)
.
Let
\mathbf{h}_{g}=\sum_{u\in V}\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell-1\right)}(u)
.
We can compute entries of
\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}(v)
bottom-up.
For the base case, it is clear that
\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}(v)[0]=\xi^{\left(0\right)}(v)
.
For
1\leq i\leq\ell
,
note that for
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}

\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{x}(v)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}(u)=\mathbf{h}_{x}[\ell-1]
and

\sum_{u\in V}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}(u)=\mathbf{h}_{g}[\ell-1]
.
Thus, since
\mathsf{hist}^{\left(\ell\right)}(v)[i]=\xi^{\left(i\right)}(v)
, it follows that

\xi^{\left(i\right)}(v)\ =\ f^{\left(i\right)}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(i\right)}\xi^{\left(i-1\right)}(v)+\sum_{x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}}\left(\mathbf{A}^{\left(i\right)}_{x}\mathbf{h}_{x}[i-1]\right)+\mathbf{R}^{\left(i\right)}\mathbf{h}_{g}[i-1]+\mathbf{b}^{\left(i\right)}\right).

We now show that for GNNs with
\mathsf{TrReLU}
activation,
the size of the history-space (and thus, in particular, the size of the spectrum) is bounded by an exponential in the description of the GNN.
Recall that in Section
4
, Lemma
4.4
,
we proved that the
\ell
-spectrum is overapproximated by the set
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{S}}^{\left(\ell\right)}}
. We now calculate the size of this set in the case of
\mathsf{TrReLU}
.
For a GNN with
\mathsf{TrReLU}
activations,
the set
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{S}}^{\left(\ell\right)}}
is
\left\{\dfrac{\mathbf{k}}{p^{\left(\ell\right)}}\middle|\ \mathbf{k}\in{\left[0\mathinner{,}\penalty p^{\left(\ell\right)}\right]}^{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}\right\}
,
where
p^{\left(\ell\right)}
is the product of the least common denominator of
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{S}}^{\left(\ell-1\right)}}
and the least common denominator of the coefficients in
\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}
,
\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{out}}
,
\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{in}}
, and
\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}
,
since the least common denominator of
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{S}}^{\left(\ell-1\right)}}
is just
p^{\left(\ell-1\right)}
in this case.
Thus we get a bound on the number of elements in the spectrum:

Lemma 5.6
.

For every
\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
and
0\leq\ell\leq L
,
the number of elements in
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{S}}^{\left(\ell\right)}}
is bounded by
\left(p^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)^{d^{(\ell)}}
.

For
0\leq\ell\leq L
, let
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{H}}}^{\left(\ell\right)}
be the Cartesian product of
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{S}}^{\left(t\right)}}
for
0\leq t\leq\ell
.
Let
D_{\mathcal{A}}=\Pi_{\ell\in{\left[L\right]}}\left(p^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)^{d^{(\ell)}}
. Note that
D_{\mathcal{A}}
is exponential in the description of the GNN.
It is obvious that the
\ell
-history-space is overapproximated by
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{H}}}^{\left(\ell\right)}
.
Thus, looking at the set
\left\{\dfrac{\mathbf{k}}{p^{\left(\ell\right)}}\middle|\ \mathbf{k}\in{\left[0\mathinner{,}\penalty p^{\left(\ell\right)}\right]}^{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}\right\}
, we can get a bound on both the number of elements in
\ell
-history-space, as well as the number of bits to represent each element:

Lemma 5.7
.

For every
\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
and
0\leq\ell\leq L
,
the number of elements in
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{H}}}^{\left(\ell\right)}
is bounded by
D_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Furthermore, every value in
{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{H}}}^{\left(\ell\right)}
is at most exponential in the size of the description of
\mathcal{A}
, hence, can be encoded with polynomially many bits.

5.2
Exponential tree model property

The previous subsection bounded the size of individual values, independent of the input graph.
We now show that whenever a GNN is satisfiable, there is
some
satisfying graph that is both reasonably small and nicely-structured. Recall that we are dealing here with directed graphs with some number of node colors.

Definition 5.8

(Trees, children, and depth)
.

An
n
-tree
is an
n
-graph such that when we remove the direction we have a tree.
For a tree
\mathcal{T}
and vertices
v,u
in
\mathcal{G}
,
we say that
u
is an out-child of
v
,
if
u
is a child of
v
and there exists an edge from
v
to
u
.
We let
\mathcal{NC}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v)
be the set of all out-children of
v
.

The depth of a vertex
v
in a tree is the distance to the root.
The depth of a tree is the maximal depth of any vertex.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a GNN with
L
layers.
Let
\mathcal{T}
be a tree with depth
L

and
v
be a vertex in
\mathcal{G}
with depth
\ell
.
We define
\mathcal{HC}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v)
as the set of
(L-\ell-1)
-histories of out-children of
v
.
Formally,
\mathcal{HC}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v):=\left\{\mathsf{hist}^{\left(L-\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(u)\middle|\ u\in\mathcal{NC}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v)\right\}
.
We define
\mathcal{NC}_{\text{in},\mathcal{G}}(v)
,
\mathcal{HC}_{\text{in},\mathcal{G}}(v)
for in-children analogously.
When the graph
\mathcal{G}
is clear from the context, we omit it and simply write

\mathcal{NC}_{\text{out}}(v)
,
\mathcal{HC}_{\text{out}}(v)
,
\mathcal{NC}_{\text{in}}(v)
, and
\mathcal{HC}_{\text{in}}(v)
.

We can now state the
exponential tree model property
.

Theorem 5.9
.

There are constants
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, so that
for every
n
-
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
with
L
layers,
if
\mathcal{A}
is satisfiable,
then there is an
n
-tree
\mathcal{T}
with depth at most
L
and root
v_{r}
satisfying the following conditions.

1.

(tree model property)
\mathcal{A}
accepts
\left\langle\mathcal{T},v_{r}\right\rangle
.

2.

(small degree property) For every vertex
v
in
\mathcal{T}
of depth
\ell
,
for
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}
, the size of
\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
is at most
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
,
and for each
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
, the number of
x
-children of
v
with
(L-\ell-1)
-history
\mathbf{h}
is at most
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
, where

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\ :=

\displaystyle c_{1}d_{\mathcal{A}}\log\left(c_{2}d_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{A}}\right),

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathcal{A}}\ :=

\displaystyle c_{1}d_{\mathcal{A}}\left(d_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{A}}\right)^{c_{2}d_{\mathcal{A}}},

d_{\mathcal{A}}:=\sum_{\ell\in{\left[L\right]}}{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
,
and
M_{\mathcal{A}}
is the product of
2D_{\mathcal{A}}
and the maximal numerator of coefficients in
\mathcal{A}
.

We prove the theorem in two steps.
First, we show that if a local GNN is satisfiable,
then it has a tree model.
We construct such tree model by an
unravelling procedure
, a common tool for modal and description logics.

Definition 5.10
.

For a graph
\mathcal{G}
,
we say a sequence
\mathfrak{P}=v_{0}R_{1}v_{1}R_{2}v_{2}\cdots v_{\ell}
is a
path
in
\mathcal{G}

if
\mathfrak{P}
satisfies the following conditions.

•

For every
0\leq t\leq\ell
,

v_{t}\in V
.

•

For every
1\leq t\leq\ell
,

R_{t}\in\left\{\rightarrow,\leftarrow\right\}
.

•

For every
1\leq t\leq\ell
,
if
R_{t}
is
\rightarrow
,
then there is an edge from
v_{t-1}
to
v_{t}
.
Otherwise, if
R_{t}
is
\leftarrow
,
then there is an edge from
v_{t}
to
v_{t-1}
.

We say that
\mathfrak{P}
is
proper
if it has no contiguous subsequence of the form

v\leftarrow u\rightarrow v
or
v\rightarrow u\leftarrow v
.

Definition 5.11
.

For a path
\mathfrak{P}=v_{0}R_{1}v_{1}R_{2}v_{2}\cdots v_{\ell}
,

\ell
is called the
length
of
\mathfrak{P}
;

v_{0}
is called the
source
of
\mathfrak{P}
;

v_{\ell}
is called the
destination
of
\mathfrak{P}
.

v_{1}
v_{2}
v_{3}
v_{4}
\mathcal{G}_{0}
v_{1}
v_{2}
v_{3}
v_{4}
v_{4}
v_{2}
v_{3}
v_{1}
v_{1}
{\mathsf{urv}^{3}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{0},v_{1}\right)

Figure 1:
Example of a
3
-unravelling tree of
\mathcal{G}_{0}
on
v_{1}
.

For example, considering the graph
\mathcal{G}_{0}
in Fig.
1
,

v_{3}\leftarrow v_{1}\rightarrow v_{4}\rightarrow v_{1}
is a proper path in
\mathcal{G}_{0}
with length 3, source
v_{3}
, and destination
v_{1}
.
However
v_{3}\leftarrow v_{1}\rightarrow v_{4}\leftarrow v_{1}
is a path in
\mathcal{G}_{0}
but not a proper path.

Definition 5.12
.

For an
n
-graph
\mathcal{G}
and a vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}
,
the
\ell
-unravelling tree
of
\mathcal{G}
on
v
,
denoted by
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
,
is the
n
-graph defined as follows.

•

The set of vertices of
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
is the collection of proper paths in
\mathcal{G}

with source
v
and length at most
\ell
,
and there is exactly one vertex in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)

with length
0
, called the root.

•

For every vertex
\mathfrak{P}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)

with non-zero length, suppose

\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}Ru
,
where
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
is also a vertex in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
.
If
R
is
\rightarrow
,
then there is an edge from
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
to
\mathfrak{P}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
.
Otherwise, if
R
is
\leftarrow
,
then there is an edge from
\mathfrak{P}
to
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
.

•

For every vertex
\mathfrak{P}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
,
the label of
\mathfrak{P}
is exactly the label of its destination.

Note that we will use symbols like
\mathfrak{P}
to refer to paths, even when they
are considered as vertices in the unravelling.
It is obvious that
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
is a tree, and
the depth is at most
L
.
For every vertex
\mathfrak{P}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)

the depth of
\mathfrak{P}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{\ell}}\left(\mathcal{G},v\right)
is the length of
\mathfrak{P}
.

Lemma 5.13
.

For every
n
-
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
,
for every
n
-graph
\mathcal{G}
and vertex
v_{r}
in
\mathcal{G}
,

\mathsf{hist}^{\left(L\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}\left(\mathfrak{P}_{r}\right)=\mathsf{hist}^{\left(L\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{r})
,
where
\mathfrak{P}_{r}
is the root of
{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)
.

Proof 5.14
.

We will prove the following stronger property:
for
0\leq\ell\leq L
,
for every vertex
\mathfrak{P}
in
{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)
with destination
v
,
if the depth of
\mathfrak{P}
is less than or equal to
L-\ell
,
then
\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
.
Note that the depth of the root vertex
\mathfrak{P}_{r}
is
0
.
So the lemma follows from the property directly.

We prove the property by induction on
\ell
.
For the base case
\ell=0
,

\xi^{\left(0\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P})
is just the label of
\mathfrak{P}
.
By the definition of the unravelling graph,
the label of
\mathfrak{P}
is the label of its destination.

For the inductive step
1\leq\ell\leq L
,
since
L-\ell<L-(\ell-1)
,
by the induction hypothesis,

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
.
Note that the GNN we considered is local, that is,
the value of
\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
only depends on

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
and
summations of
\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(u)
for its outgoing and incoming neighbors
u
. Thus, it is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{out},{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}}(\mathfrak{P})}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(u)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(u)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{in},{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}}(\mathfrak{P})}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{in},\mathcal{G}}(u)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(u).

Suppose that
\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}_{r}
.
For every
u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v_{r})
,
by the definition of the unravelling tree,

\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}=v_{r}\rightarrow u
is a vertex in
{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)
.
Furthermore, there is an edge from
\mathfrak{P}_{r}
to
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
.
Note that the depth of
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
is
1\leq L-(\ell-1)
.
Thus, by the induction hypothesis,
for every
u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v_{r})
,

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(u)
,
which implies that the summation of
(\ell-1)
-features of outgoing neighbors of
\mathfrak{P}_{r}
is the same as
v_{r}
.
We can treat the incoming neighbors analogously.

If
\mathfrak{P}\neq\mathfrak{P}_{r}
,
suppose that
\mathfrak{P}
decomposes into a concatenation of
a path
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
with destination
v_{p}

and a vertex
v
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}_{p}\leftarrow v
.
For every
u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{out},\mathcal{G}}(v)
,
if
u\neq v_{p}
,
then
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow u
is a vertex in
{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)
.
By an argument similar to the previous case,
we can show that

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(u)
.
Note that
\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow v_{p}
is not a vertex in
\mathcal{G}^{L}_{v_{r}}
,
because it is not a proper path.
However,
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
is a vertex in
{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)
and
there is an edge from
\mathfrak{P}
to
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
.
Thus
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
is also a outgoing neighbor of
\mathfrak{P}
.
Since the depth of
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
is less than
\mathfrak{P}
,
the depth of
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
is less than
L-(\ell-1)
.
Hence

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G},v_{r}\right)}(\mathfrak{P}_{p})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{p})
.
Thus, it holds that
the summation of
(\ell-1)
-features of outgoing neighbors of
\mathfrak{P}
is the same as
v
.
We can treat the incoming neighbors analogously without considering
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
,
since
\mathfrak{P}_{p}
is not an incoming neighbors of
\mathfrak{P}
.

Next, we show that for every tree model,
we can apply some surgery and obtain another tree model whose size is bounded.

Definition 5.15
.

For every
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
,
for every
n
-tree
\mathcal{G}
and
v
in
\mathcal{G}
with depth
\ell
,
the
characteristic system of
v
(w.r.t.
\mathcal{A}
),
denoted by
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
,
is a linear constraint system with variables

\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
defined as follows.
For
1\leq t\leq L-\ell
and
1\leq i\leq{d^{\left(t\right)}}
,

D_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(t\right)}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v)+\mathbf{p}^{\left(t\right)}(v)+\sum_{x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}}\mathbf{A}^{\left(t\right)}_{x}\sum_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)}z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\cdot\mathbf{h}[t-1]+\mathbf{b}^{\left(t\right)}\right)_{i}\ \circledast_{t,i}\ D_{\mathcal{A}}\xi^{\left(t\right)}_{i}(v)

is a constraint in
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
,
where

\mathbf{p}^{\left(t\right)}(v)\ =\ \begin{cases}0,&\text{if $v=v_{r}$}\\
\mathbf{A}^{\left(t\right)}_{\text{out}}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v_{p}),&\text{if there is an edge from $v$ to $v$'s parent $v_{p}$}\\
\mathbf{A}^{\left(t\right)}_{\text{in}}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v_{p}),&\text{if there is an edge from $v$'s parent $v_{p}$ to $v$}.\end{cases}

If
\xi^{\left(t\right)}_{i}(v)=1
, then
\circledast_{t,i}
is
\geq
;
if
\xi^{\left(t\right)}_{i}(v)=0
, then
\circledast_{t,i}
is
\leq
;
otherwise,
\circledast_{t,i}
is
=
.

A solution to the system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
where each variable
z_{x,\mathbf{h}}
is assigned the value
a_{x,\mathbf{h}}\in\mathbb{N}
is denoted by

\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\leftarrow a_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
.

We remark that the multiplication by the factor
D_{\mathcal{A}}
is to ensure the coefficients in the constraint are all integers.
Note that there are at most
d_{\mathcal{A}}=\sum_{\ell\in{\left[L\right]}}{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
constraints.
Because
\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\subseteq{\uparrow\!{\mathcal{H}}}^{\left(L-\ell\right)}
,
by Lemma
5.7
,

\left|\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\right|\leq D_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Thus, there are at most
2D_{\mathcal{A}}
variables in the system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
.
Since
\xi^{\left(t\right)}_{i}(v)
is the output of
\mathsf{TrReLU}
,
0\leq\xi^{\left(t\right)}_{i}(v)\leq 1
.
Therefore, the absolute value of the coefficients in the system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
is bounded by
the product of
2D_{\mathcal{A}}
and the maximal numerator of coefficients in
\mathcal{A}
:
that is, the values are bounded by
M_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Next, we will show that the number of children
of each node in the tree can be bounded.
We will use the following lemma, which stems from
[
8
,
19
]
, see also
[
15
, Corollary 2.2]
.

Lemma 5.16
.

There are constants
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every linear constraint system
\mathcal{Q}
,
if
\mathcal{Q}
admits a solution in
\mathbb{N}
, then it admits a solution in
\mathbb{N}
in which
the number of variables assigned non-zero values is at most
c_{1}t\log\left(c_{2}tM\right)
and
every variable is assigned a value at most
c_{1}t(tM)^{c_{2}t}
,
where
t
is the number of constraints in
\mathcal{Q}
and
M
is the maximal absolute value of the coefficient in
\mathcal{Q}
.

We will use Lemma
5.16
above to show that
every satisfiable
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
is satisfiable by a small tree model in which the values of the feature vector of each node and the number of children of each node is at most exponential in the length of the description of the GNN, as stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
,
\mathcal{G}
an
n
-tree and
v
in
\mathcal{G}
with depth
\ell
, Let

\mathcal{Q}_{v}
be the characteristic system of equations of
v
with respect to
\mathcal{A}
.
Then
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
has a solution in
\mathbb{N}
such that
the number of variables assigned to non-zero values is at most
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
,
and every variable is assigned to a value
bounded by
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
,
where
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
are as defined in Theorem
5.1
.

Proof 5.18
.

We first prove that
\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\leftarrow a_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
is a solution of the characteristic system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
,
where
a_{x,\mathbf{h}}
is the number of
v
’s
x
-children whose
(L-\ell-1)
-history is
\mathbf{h}
.

Without loss of generality,
suppose
v
has a parent
v_{p}
and there exists an edge from
v
to
v_{p}
.
The other two cases, i.e., when the edge is oriented from
v_{p}
to
v
or when the
v
is the root node, can be treated analogously.

Note that for
1\leq t\leq L-\ell
,

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{out}}(v)}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(u)\ =

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v_{p})+\sum_{u\in\mathcal{NC}_{\text{out}}(v)}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(u)

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v_{p})+\sum_{u\in\mathcal{NC}_{\text{out}}(v)}\left(\mathsf{hist}^{\left(L-\ell-1\right)}(u)\right)[t-1]

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v_{p})+\sum_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{\text{out}}(v)}a_{\text{out},\mathbf{h}}\cdot\mathbf{h}[t-1]

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\text{in}}(v)}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(u)\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{\text{in}}(v)}a_{\text{in},\mathbf{h}}\cdot\mathbf{h}[t-1].

Combining with the definition of
\xi^{\left(t\right)}(v)
,

\xi^{\left(t\right)}(v)=\mathsf{TrReLU}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(t\right)}\xi^{\left(t-1\right)}(v)+\mathbf{p}^{\left(t\right)}(v)+\sum_{x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}}\mathbf{A}^{\left(t\right)}_{x}\sum_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)}a_{x,\mathbf{h}}\cdot\mathbf{h}[t-1]+\mathbf{b}^{\left(t\right)}\right).

By the definition of
\mathsf{TrReLU}
, we can verify that

\left\{z_{x,h}\leftarrow a_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
is a solution of the characteristic system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
.

Recall that there are
d_{\mathcal{A}}=\sum_{\ell\in{\left[L\right]}}{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
constraints in
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
and
the absolute value of each coefficient is bounded by
M_{\mathcal{A}}
.
By Lemma
5.16
,
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
has a
small
solution

\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\leftarrow a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
: that is, a solution
where the number of variables assigned to non-zero values is at most
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
,
and every variable is assigned to a value
bounded by
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Thus
a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}
is the desired solution of the lemma.

Finally, we are ready to prove Theorem
5.9
.

Proof 5.19
.

The constants
c_{1},c_{2}
are the same constants as in Lemma
5.16
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a satisfiable
n
-
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
with
L
layers
which satisfied by
\left\langle\mathcal{G}_{0},v\right\rangle
.
Let us denote the
L
-unravelling tree
{\mathsf{urv}^{L}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{0},v\right)
by
\mathcal{T}
and the root of
\mathcal{T}
by
v_{r}
.
By Lemma
5.13
,
\mathcal{A}
also accept
\left\langle\mathcal{T},v_{r}\right\rangle
.
This proves the tree model property as required by the theorem.
We will show how to convert it into another tree
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
that satisfies the small degree property in the theorem. That is, we show that
for every vertex
v
in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
,
for
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}
, the size of
\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
is at most
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
,
and for each
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
, the number of
x
-children of
v
with
(L-\ell-1)
-history
\mathbf{h}
is at most
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
, where

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\ :=

\displaystyle c_{1}d_{\mathcal{A}}\log\left(c_{2}d_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{A}}\right),

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathcal{A}}\ :=

\displaystyle c_{1}d_{\mathcal{A}}\left(d_{\mathcal{A}}M_{\mathcal{A}}\right)^{c_{2}d_{\mathcal{A}}},

d_{\mathcal{A}}:=\sum_{\ell\in{\left[L\right]}}{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
,
and
M_{\mathcal{A}}
is the product of
2D_{\mathcal{A}}
and the maximal numerator of coefficients in
\mathcal{A}
.

To this end,
we say that a vertex
u
in
\mathcal{T}
is a
bad vertex

if
u
does not satisfy the small degree property of the theorem, i.e.,
there is
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}
such that
at least one of the following conditions holds.

•

The size of
\mathcal{HC}_{x}(u)
is strictly bigger than
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
.

•

There is
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(u)
where the number of
x
-children of
u
with
(L-\ell-1)
-history
\mathbf{h}
is strictly bigger than
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
.

If there is no bad vertex, then
\left\langle\mathcal{T},v_{r}\right\rangle
is the desired
n
-tree model of the theorem.

Suppose there is a bad vertex in
\mathcal{T}
.
Let
v
be a bad vertex with the maximal depth
\ell
in
\mathcal{T}
.
Let
a_{x,\mathbf{h}}
be the number of
v
’s
x
-children in
\mathcal{T}
whose
(L-\ell-1)
-history is
\mathbf{h}
.

Consider the characteristic system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
.
Obviously,
\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\leftarrow a_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
is a solution to the system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
.
By Lemma
5.17
,

\mathcal{Q}_{v}
has a
small
solution
\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\leftarrow a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}

where the number of variables assigned to non-zero values is at most
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
,
and every value
a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}
is
at most
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}=0
whenever
a_{x,\mathbf{h}}=0
,
for every
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}

and
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
be the
n
-tree obtained by applying the following modification to
\mathcal{T}
.
For every
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}
and
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
,
let
u_{\mathbf{h}}
be a
x
-child of
v
whose
(L-\ell-1)
-history is
\mathbf{h}
.
We first remove all children of
v
.
Then for every
x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}
and
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
,
we duplicate
a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}
copies of the subtree induced by
u_{\mathbf{h}}

and attach each subtree copy as an
x
-child of
v
.
If
x
is
out
, we add an edge from
v
to the root of the duplicated subtrees.
Otherwise, if
x
is
in
, we add an edge from the root of the duplicated subtrees to
v
.

Obviously the number of
v
’s
x
-children in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
with
(L-\ell-1)
-history
\mathbf{h}
is
a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}
,
which is bounded by
\beta_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Moreover, the size
\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)
is bounded
by the number of variables in
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
that are assigned non-zero values, which is bounded by
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Thus, the vertex
v
is no longer a bad vertex in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, since
\left\{z_{x,\mathbf{h}}\leftarrow a^{\prime}_{x,\mathbf{h}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\left\{\text{out},\text{in}\right\}\\
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{HC}_{x}(v)\end{subarray}}
is the solution to the system
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
,
the
(L-\ell)
-history of
v
in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is the same
as in
\mathcal{T}
.

Finally, note that
v
is a bad vertex with the maximal depth in
\mathcal{T}
.
Hence every descendant of
v
in
\mathcal{T}
is not a bad vertex in
\mathcal{T}
.
Since the only difference between
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is that
we make duplicates of the subtrees rooted at the children of
v
,
every descendant of
v
in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is also not a bad vertex in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.
Therefore the number of bad vertices in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is one less than
\mathcal{T}
.
We can repeat the procedure until there are no more bad vertices and the desired
n
-tree model is obtained.

5.3
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-completeness for
the satisfiability problem of
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
5.1
.
Intuitively, in Theorem
5.9
,
we showed that every satisfiable
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
has an exponential size tree model.
The theorem did not say anything about the size of the numbers in features. But
Lemma
5.7
tells us that for every graph the size of computed features is not very large.

Since the size of the model may be exponential, the naïve algorithm, which guesses the whole model and checks it, takes nondeterministic exponential time.
However, we can reduce to
\mathsf{PSPACE}
using the same approach as in the
\mathsf{PSPACE}
bound for modal logic
[
14
]
: in order to check the validity of the tree model, it is sufficient to check the validity of each vertex locally.
That is, we only need to compute the history from its parent and children and check this value.
Thus the following Algorithm
1
decides the satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s by guessing children of a vertex and checking one by procedure
Check
(
\mathcal{A}
,
\ell
,
x
,
\mathbf{h}_{p}
,
\mathbf{h}
) at a time.
For the base case in line 7, if the depth of the vertex
v
is
L
, meaning that
v
is a leaf vertex of the tree,
then the history
\mathbf{h}
is always satisfiable by assigning it to the label of
v
.
Otherwise, the procedure guesses the label
e
of
v
in line 9,
the lower-level history of its children in line 10,
and the number of children of each history in line 11.
While the number of children may be exponential, most of them share the same history.
More precisely, it suffices to guess a polynomial number of distinct histories and a polynomial number of exponential counts, the sizes of which remain polynomial.
After that, the procedure checks the validity of each child’s history by recursively calling itself in lines 12 to 16.
Finally, it verifies the correctness of the guess by computing the history and comparing it to
\mathbf{h}
in line 17, as described in Lemma
5.4
.
Since the depth of the recursive calls is at most
L
,
Algorithm
1
is a nondeterministic polynomial-space algorithm.
The correctness follows from Theorem
5.9
and Lemma
5.7
.

\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s

\mathbf{h}_{\text{in},i}

The lower bound is established by embedding the description logic
\mathcal{ALC}
into
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
.
Since the concept satisfiability problem of
\mathcal{ALC}
with one role is
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-hard
[
21
]
,
it will follow from the embedding that the finite satisfiability problem of
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
is also
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-hard.
Since the reduction from
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
to
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
mentioned in Theorem
4.10
is polynomial,
the satisfiability problem of
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
is also
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-hard, and so it
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
.

Lemma 5.20
.

There exists a polynomial time translation
\pi_{x}
from
\mathcal{ALC}
concepts with one role
R
to
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
formulas such that the
\mathcal{ALC}
concept
C
is satisfiable if and only if the
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
formula
\pi_{x}(C)
is finitely satisfiable.

Proof 5.21
.

We will define
\pi_{x}
and
\pi_{y}
, which are the standard translation from
\mathcal{ALC}
concepts to first-order logic formulas, with some slight modification to quantifiers to fit our logic.
It is routine to check that the
\mathcal{ALC}
concept
C
is satisfiable if and only if
\pi_{x}(C)
is finitely satisfiable.

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(A)\ =

\displaystyle A(x)

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(A)\ =

\displaystyle A(y)

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(\neg C)\ =

\displaystyle\neg\pi_{x}(C)

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(\neg C)\ =

\displaystyle\neg\pi_{y}(C)

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(C\sqcap D)\ =

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(C)\land\pi_{x}(D)

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(C\sqcap D)\ =

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(C)\land\pi_{y}(D)

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(C\sqcup D)\ =

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(C)\lor\pi_{x}(D)

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(C\sqcup D)\ =

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(C)\lor\pi_{y}(D)

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(\exists R.C)\ =

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\#_{y}\left[E(x,y)\land\pi_{y}(C)\right]}\geq 1

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(\exists R.C)\ =

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\#_{x}\left[E(y,x)\land\pi_{x}(C)\right]}\geq 1

\displaystyle\pi_{x}(\forall R.C)\ =

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\#_{y}\left[E(x,y)\land\neg\pi_{y}(C)\right]}=0

\displaystyle\pi_{y}(\forall R.C)\ =

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\#_{x}\left[E(y,x)\land\neg\pi_{x}(C)\right]}=0

5.4
\mathsf{NP}
-completeness for the satisfiability problem of fixed layer
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s

Let us consider the run time of Algorithm
1
.
The procedure
Check
calls itself
2\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
times.
The depth of recursion is
L
.
It takes only polynomial time to compute the history from the parent and children.
Hence the runtime of the algorithm is proportional to

1+2\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}+\left(2\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\right)^{2}+\cdots+\left(2\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\right)^{L}\ =\ \frac{\left(2\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\right)^{L+1}-1}{2\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}-1}.

Note that
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
is polynomial in the description of
\mathcal{A}
. Thus for fixed layer
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
, Algorithm
1
only takes nondeterministic polynomial time.

We show that when the number of layers is fixed, there exists a polynominal time reduction from
{3\text{-}\mathsf{SAT}}

to the satisfiability problem of fixed layer
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
.
Since
{3\text{-}\mathsf{SAT}}
is
\mathsf{NP}
-hard, so is the satisfiability problem of fixed layer
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
and
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
.

Lemma 5.22
.

There exists a polynomial time reduction from 3-CNF formulas
\varphi
to 2-layer
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}
such that
\varphi
is satisfiable if and only if
\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}
is satisfiable.

Proof 5.23
.

Let
\varphi
be a 3-CNF formula with
n
propositional variables and
m
clauses.

\varphi\ :=\ (\ell_{11}\lor\ell_{12}\lor\ell_{13})\land(\ell_{21}\lor\ell_{22}\lor\ell_{23})\land\cdots\land(\ell_{m1}\lor\ell_{m2}\lor\ell_{m3})

We define the
2
-layer
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}
as follows.
The dimensions
{d^{\left(0\right)}}=n
,
{d^{\left(1\right)}}=m
, and
{d^{\left(2\right)}}=1
.
The coefficient matrices and bias vectors are chosen so that the features of
\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}
satisfies the following conditions.
For the first layer,
for
1\leq t\leq m
and
1\leq j\leq 3
, let

\displaystyle g(t,j)\ :=\ \begin{cases}\xi^{\left(0\right)}_{s}(v),&\text{if $\ell_{tj}=x_{s}$}\\
1-\xi^{\left(0\right)}_{s}(v),&\text{if $\ell_{tj}=\neg x_{s}$},\end{cases}

and
\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)=\mathsf{TrReLU}\left(g(t,1)+g(t,2)+g(t,3)\right)
.
For the second layer,

\xi^{\left(2\right)}_{1}(v)=\mathsf{TrReLU}\left(\sum_{t\in{\left[m\right]}}\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)-m+1\right)
.

Suppose
\varphi
is satisfied by the assignment
\left\{x_{i}\leftarrow a_{i}\right\}_{i\in{\left[n\right]}}
,
we define the
n
-graph
\mathcal{G}
as follows.
The graph
\mathcal{G}
has only one vertex
v
.
For
1\leq i\leq n
,
\xi^{\left(0\right)}_{i}(v)=a_{i}
.
It is not difficult to check that for
1\leq t\leq m
,
for
1\leq j\leq 3
,
if
\ell_{tj}
is satisfied by the assignment,
then
g(t,j)=1
.
Otherwise,
g(t,j)=0
.
For the first layer, for
1\leq t\leq m
,
since the
t^{th}
clause is satisfied,
there exists some
1\leq j\leq 3
,
such that
\ell_{tj}
is satisfied,
which implies that
\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)=1
.
For the second layer,
note that all clauses are satisfied.
Therefore
\sum_{t\in{\left[m\right]}}\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)=m
,
which implies that
\xi^{\left(2\right)}_{1}(v)=1
.
Thus
\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}
is satisfied by
\left\langle\mathcal{G},v\right\rangle
.

Suppose
\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}
is satisfied by
\left\langle\mathcal{G},v\right\rangle
.
We claim that
\left\{x_{i}\leftarrow\xi^{\left(0\right)}_{i}(v)\right\}_{i\in{\left[n\right]}}
is a satisfying assignment for
\varphi
.
Note that for
1\leq t\leq m
,
\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)
is either 0 or 1.
Thus if
\xi^{\left(2\right)}_{1}(v)\geq 1/2
, then
\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)=1
for all
1\leq t\leq m
.
Since
\xi^{\left(1\right)}_{t}(v)=1
,
there exists
1\leq j\leq 3
, such that
g(t,j)=1
,
which implies that
\ell_{tj}
is satisfied by the assignment. So the
t^{th}
clause is satisfied by the assignment.
Since all clauses are satisfied by the assignment,

\varphi
is satisfied by
\left\{x_{i}\leftarrow\xi^{\left(0\right)}_{i}(v)\right\}_{i\in{\left[n\right]}}
.

6
GNNs with unbounded activation functions

In this section, we consider GNNs with unbounded activations, such as the standard
\mathsf{ReLU}
. Since we have already shown that global aggregation leads
to undecidability even in the bounded case, in this section
we will only deal with GNNs that lack global aggregation
.
In Section
6.1
,
we introduce a logic that also helps with understanding expressiveness of this class of GNNs.
In Section
6.2
,
we show that the satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{O}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
and
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
are undecidable, a contrast to the case with eventually constant activation functions.
In Section
6.3
,
we prove undecidability for universal satisfiability problems.

In Section
6.4
,
we turn to the satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
, and give a positive result about decidability.
We will use the idea of representing the possible values of activation, which was also utilized in the case of decidability for eventually constant activations. But in this case, we will be representing an infinite set of values, using Presburger formulas.

6.1
Logics for unbounded activation function GNNs and separation of expressiveness

We will prove that one can express more with unbounded activations then with bounded ones. We will use logical characterizations of these GNNs as a tool for this separation, and we will also use them in the next subsection to obtain undecidability results. We will not obtain an expressiveness characterization of GNNs with unbounded activations, but merely a logic that embeds in
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s: local two-variable modal logic with two-hop Presburger quantifiers (
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
),
which is the extension of
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
where the guards are conjunctions of at most two binary predicates.

Definition 6.1
.

The syntax of
local two-variable modal logic with two-hop Presburger quantifiers
(
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
) over vocabulary
\tau
is defined inductively:

•

\top
is a
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula.

•

for a unary predicate
U\in\tau
,
U(x)
is a
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula.

•

if
\varphi(x)
is a
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula, then so is
\neg\varphi(x)
.

•

if
\varphi_{1}(x)
and
\varphi_{2}(x)
are
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formulas,
then so is
\varphi_{1}(x)\land\varphi_{2}(x)
.

•

if
\left\{\varphi_{t}(x)\right\}_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}
and

\left\{\varphi_{t}^{\prime}(x)\right\}_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}

are sets of
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formulas,

\left\{\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)\right\}_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}
is a set of guard formulas, each of form

{E(x,z)\land E(z,y)}
,

{E(x,z)\land E(y,z)}
,

{E(z,x)\land E(z,y)}
, or

{E(z,x)\land E(y,z)}
,
and

\left\{\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}(x,y)\right\}_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}
is another set of guard formulas, each of form
E(x,y)
or
E(y,x)
,
then

\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\lambda_{t}\cdot{\#_{z,y}\left[\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)\land\varphi_{t}(y)\right]}+\sum_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}\lambda_{t}^{\prime}\cdot{\#_{y}\left[\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}(x,y)\land\varphi_{t}^{\prime}(y)\right]}\ \circledast\ \delta

is also a
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula.
The numbers
\delta,\lambda_{t}
,
\lambda^{\prime}_{t}
, and the comparison
\circledast
are as in the standard Presburger quantifier definition.

The idea is that we can still count a linear combination of cardinalities of the number of nodes satisfying a given lower-level formula that are one-hop away from the current node – as in
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
. Optionally, we can add on a linear combination of the number of two-hop paths that lead to a
node satisfying other lower-level formulas.

The semantics of the formulas is given inductively, with the only step that is different from the usual cases being for the quantification, which is the obvious one. We call these
two-hop Presburger quantifiers
.
We can apply a similar proof technique as in Theorem
4.17

to show that every
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula is expressible using a
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

For every
n
-
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula
\Psi(x)
,
there exists an
n
-
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}_{\Psi}
,
such that
\Psi(x)
and
\mathcal{A}_{\Psi}
are equivalent.

For every
n
-
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula
\Psi(x)
, let
L
be the number of subformulas of
\Psi(x)
and
\left\{\varphi_{\ell}(x)\right\}_{\ell\in{\left[L\right]}}
be an enumeration of subformulas of
\Psi(x)
satisfying that

\varphi_{L}(x)
is
\Psi(x)

and for each
\varphi_{i}(x)
and
\varphi_{j}(x)
, if
\varphi_{i}(x)
is a strict subformula of
\varphi_{j}(x)
, then
i<j
.

We define the
(3L+1)
-layer
n
-
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}_{\Psi}
as follows.
The input dimension
{d^{\left(0\right)}}
is
n
.
For
1\leq\ell\leq 3L
, the dimension
{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
is
L+K+n
, and
{d^{\left(3L+1\right)}}=1
,
where
K
is the maximum
k
in any two-hop Presburger quantifier within
\Psi(x)
.
The coefficient matrices and bias vectors will be chosen so that the features of
\mathcal{A}_{\Psi}
satisfy the following conditions.

Firstly, for
1\leq\ell\leq L
,

\xi^{\left(3L\right)}_{\ell}(v)\ =\ \xi^{\left(3L-1\right)}_{\ell}(v)\ =\ \cdots\ =\ \xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v).

The condition of
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)
depends on the formula
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
.

•

If
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
is
\top
,
then
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)=1
.

•

If
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
is
U_{j}(x)
for some unary predicate
U_{j}
,
then
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)=\xi^{\left(3\ell-1\right)}_{L+K+j}(v)
.

•

If
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
is
\neg\varphi_{j}(x)
,
then
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)=\mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\xi^{\left(3\ell-1\right)}_{j}(v)\right)
.

•

If
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
is
\varphi_{j_{1}}(x)\land\varphi_{j_{2}}(x)
,
then
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)=\mathsf{ReLU}\left(\xi^{\left(3\ell-1\right)}_{j_{1}}(v)+\xi^{\left(3\ell-1\right)}_{j_{2}}(v)-1\right)
.

•

If
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
is

\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\lambda_{t}\cdot{\#_{z,y}\left[\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)\land\varphi_{j_{t}}(y)\right]}+\sum_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}\lambda_{t}^{\prime}\cdot{\#_{y}\left[\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}(x,y)\land\varphi_{j_{t}^{\prime}}(y)\right]}\ \circledast\ \delta,

then

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)\ =

\displaystyle\mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\xi^{\left(3\ell-1\right)}_{\ell}(v)\right)

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(3\ell-1\right)}_{\ell}(v)\ =

\displaystyle\mathsf{ReLU}\left(-\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\lambda_{t}\cdot\sum_{u\in X^{1}_{t}(v)}\xi^{\left(3\ell-2\right)}_{L+t}(u)-\sum_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}\lambda_{t}^{\prime}\cdot\sum_{u\in X^{\prime}_{t}(v)}\xi^{\left(3\ell-2\right)}_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)+\delta-1\right),

and, for
1\leq t\leq k
,

\xi^{\left(3\ell-2\right)}_{L+t}(v)\ =\ \mathsf{ReLU}\left(\sum_{u\in X^{2}_{t}(v)}\xi^{\left(3\ell-3\right)}_{j_{t}}(u)\right),

where

\displaystyle X^{1}_{t}(v)\ :=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}_{\text{out}}(v),&\text{if $\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)$ is $E(x,z)\land E(z,y)$ or $E(x,z)\land E(y,z)$}\\
\mathcal{N}_{\text{in}}(v),&\text{if $\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)$ is $E(z,x)\land E(z,y)$ or $E(z,x)\land E(y,z)$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle X^{2}_{t}(v)\ :=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}_{\text{out}}(v),&\text{if $\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)$ is $E(x,z)\land E(z,y)$ or $E(z,x)\land E(z,y)$}\\
\mathcal{N}_{\text{in}}(v),&\text{if $\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)$ is $E(x,z)\land E(y,z)$ or $E(z,x)\land E(y,z)$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle X^{\prime}_{t}(v)\ :=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}_{\text{out}}(v),&\text{if $\epsilon^{\prime}_{t}(x,y)$ is $E(x,y)$}\\
\mathcal{N}_{\text{in}}(v),&\text{if $\epsilon^{\prime}_{t}(x,y)$ is $E(y,x)$}.\end{cases}

Next, the conditions for the last
n
entries of features are as follows:
for
1\leq t\leq n
,

\xi^{\left(3L\right)}_{L+K+t}(v)\ =\ \xi^{\left(3L-1\right)}_{L+K+t}(v)\ =\ \cdots\ =\ \xi^{\left(1\right)}_{L+K+t}(v)\ =\ \xi^{\left(0\right)}_{t}(v).

Finally, for the last layer,
\xi^{\left(3L+1\right)}_{1}(v)=\xi^{\left(3L\right)}_{L}(v)
.

It is easy to see that there are GNNs that satisfy these conditions.

Recall that a vector
\mathbf{v}
is
\ell
-correct for
\Psi(x)
w.r.t.
\left\langle\mathcal{G},v\right\rangle
,
if, for
1\leq i\leq\ell
,
\mathbf{v}_{i}={\left\llbracket\varphi_{i}(v)\right\rrbracket}_{\mathcal{G}}
.
The theorem will follow once we have shown the following property of
\mathcal{A}_{\Psi}
:

Lemma 6.3
.

For every
n
-graph
\mathcal{G}
and vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}
,
for
1\leq\ell\leq L
, the feature

\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}(v)
of
\mathcal{A}_{\Psi}
is
\ell
-correct w.r.t.
\left\langle\mathcal{G},v\right\rangle
.

Proof 6.4
.

The proof is similar to Lemma
4.19
.
Here we only present the case where the subformula is a two-hop Presburger quantifier.

For
1\leq\ell\leq L
,
for
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
,
note that
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{i}(v)=\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}_{i}(v)
.
By the induction hypothesis,

\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}(v)
is
(\ell-1)
-correct for
\Psi(x)
w.r.t.
\left\langle\mathcal{G},v\right\rangle
,
which implies that
\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}_{i}(v)={\left\llbracket\varphi_{i}(v)\right\rrbracket}
.
Thus, it holds that
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{i}(v)={\left\llbracket\varphi_{i}(v)\right\rrbracket}
.

It remains to show the case where
i=\ell
,
Suppose that
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
is a two-hop Presburger quantifier:

\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\lambda_{t}\cdot{\#_{z,y}\left[\epsilon_{t}(x,z,y)\land\varphi_{j_{t}}(y)\right]}+\sum_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}\lambda_{t}^{\prime}\cdot{\#_{y}\left[\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}(x,y)\land\varphi_{j_{t}^{\prime}}(y)\right]}\ \circledast\ \delta.

For
1\leq t\leq k
,
because
\varphi_{j_{t}}(x)
is a strict subformula of
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
,

\ell-1\geq j_{t}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
for every vertex
u\in V
,

\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}(u)
is
(\ell-1)
-correct for
\Psi(x)
w.r.t.
\left\langle\mathcal{G},u\right\rangle
,
which implies that
\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}_{j_{t}}(u)={\left\llbracket\varphi_{j_{t}}(u)\right\rrbracket}
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\{(u,u^{\prime})\in V\times V\middle|\ \mathcal{G}\models\epsilon_{t}(v,u,u^{\prime})\land\varphi_{j_{t}}(u^{\prime})\right\}\right|\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in X^{1}_{t}(v)}\sum_{u^{\prime}\in X^{2}_{t}(u)}{\left\llbracket\varphi_{j_{t}}(u^{\prime})\right\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in X^{1}_{t}(v)}\sum_{u^{\prime}\in X^{2}_{t}(u)}\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}_{j_{t}}(u^{\prime}).

For
1\leq t\leq k^{\prime}
,
because
\varphi_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(x)
are strict subformulas of
\varphi_{\ell}(x)
,

\ell-1\geq j^{\prime}_{t}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
for every
u\in V
,

\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}(u)
is
(\ell-1)
-correct for
\Psi(x)
w.r.t.
\left\langle\mathcal{G},u\right\rangle
,
which implies that
\xi^{\left(3\ell-2\right)}_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)=\xi^{\left(3(\ell-1)\right)}_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)={\left\llbracket\varphi_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)\right\rrbracket}
.
Thus, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\left\{u\in V\middle|\ \mathcal{G}\models\epsilon^{\prime}_{t}(v,u)\land\varphi^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)\right\}\right|\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in X^{\prime}_{t}(v)}{\left\llbracket\varphi_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)\right\rrbracket}\ =\

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in X^{\prime}_{t}(v)}\xi^{\left(3\ell-2\right)}_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u).

Let
w_{2}(v)
and
w_{1}(v)
be values defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{2}(v)\ :=

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\lambda_{t}\cdot\left|\left\{(u,u^{\prime})\in V\times V\middle|\ \mathcal{G}\models\epsilon_{t}(v,u,u^{\prime})\land\varphi_{j_{t}}(u^{\prime})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\lambda_{t}\cdot\left(\sum_{u\in X^{1}_{t}(v)}\sum_{u^{\prime}\in X^{2}_{t}(u)}\xi^{\left(3\ell-3\right)}_{L+t}(u)\right)

\displaystyle w_{1}(v)\ :=

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}\lambda^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\left|\left\{u\in V\middle|\ \mathcal{G}\models\epsilon^{\prime}_{t}(v,u)\land\varphi_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in{\left[k^{\prime}\right]}}\lambda^{\prime}_{t}\cdot\sum_{u\in X^{\prime}_{t}(v)}\xi^{\left(3\ell-2\right)}_{j^{\prime}_{t}}(u).

Observe that

\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)\ =\ \mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\mathsf{ReLU}\left(-w_{2}(v)-w_{1}(v)+\delta-1\right)\right).

By the semantics of two-hop Presburger quantifiers,
if
\mathcal{G}\models\varphi_{\ell}(v)
, then
w_{1}(v)+w_{2}(v)\geq\delta
,
which implies
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)=1
.
On the other hand,
if
\mathcal{G}\not\models\varphi_{\ell}(v)
, then
w_{1}(v)+w_{2}(v)<\delta
,
which implies
\xi^{\left(3\ell\right)}_{\ell}(v)=0
.

Theorem
6.2
follows easily from Lemma
6.3
.

We can use the logic to get an expressiveness separation for GNNs.
By Theorem
6.2

to show that
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s can do more than
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s,
it is sufficient to show that there is a
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula that is not given by any
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
:

Lemma 6.5
.

\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
is strictly more expressive than
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s.

We claim that the property “the number of two-hop paths from the vertex
v
to the green vertices is the same as the number of two-hop paths from the vertex
v
to the blue vertices” gives the separation. It is easy to express
in the two-hop logic. To show that no
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
can express it, we construct a sequence of pairs of graphs, each with a special node, such that the property holds
in the special node of the first graph and fails in the special node of the second, while for every
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
,
for any sufficiently large pairs of graphs in this sequence,
the special nodes are indistinguishable by
\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 6.6
.

For
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
,
the
(n_{1},n_{2})
-bipolar graph

\left\langle V,E,U_{1},U_{2}\right\rangle
is a
2
-graph defined as follows,
see Fig.
2
.

\displaystyle U_{1}\ :=

\displaystyle\left\{v_{1,i}\middle|\ 1\leq i\leq n_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle U_{2}\ :=

\displaystyle\left\{v_{2,i}\middle|\ 1\leq i\leq n_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle V\ :=

\displaystyle U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup\left\{v_{0},v_{1},v_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle E\ :=

\displaystyle\left\{(v_{0},v_{1}),(v_{0},v_{2})\right\}\cup\left\{(v_{1},v_{1,i})\middle|\ 1\leq i\leq n_{1}\right\}\cup\left\{(v_{2},v_{2,i})\middle|\ 1\leq i\leq n_{2}\right\}

v_{0}
v_{2}
v_{1}
v_{2,1}
v_{2,2}
\vdots
v_{2,n_{2}}
v_{1,1}
v_{1,2}
\vdots
v_{1,n_{1}}

Figure 2
:
(n_{1},n_{2})
-bipolar graph.

We first show that
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s cannot distinguish sufficiently large pairs of bipolar graphs.

Lemma 6.7
.

For each
2
-
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
,
there exist a threshold
n_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbb{N}
,
such that for every
{n_{1},n_{2}\geq n_{\mathcal{A}}}
,
the following properties hold.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be the
(n_{\mathcal{A}},n_{\mathcal{A}})
-bipolar graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be the
(n_{1},n_{2})
-bipolar graph.
For every
0\leq\ell\leq L
,

1.

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0})
,

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1})
, and

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{2})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{2})
.

2.

for
1\leq i\leq n_{\mathcal{A}}
and
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n_{1}
,

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1,i})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1,i^{\prime}})
.

3.

for
1\leq i\leq n_{\mathcal{A}}
and
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n_{2}
,

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{2,i})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{2,i^{\prime}})
.

Proof 6.8
.

Recall from Theorem
4.2
that the spectrum of a
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
at any layer
\ell
, denoted
\mathcal{S}^{\left(\ell\right)}
, is finite.
We will show that we can compute the required threshold using the thresholds for the eventually constant activations along with the maximum rational number in the spectrum.

For
1\leq\ell\leq L
and
1\leq i\leq{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
,
for
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\in\mathcal{S}^{\left(\ell-1\right)}
, let

\displaystyle q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}\right)\ :=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{out}}\mathbf{s}_{1}\right)_{i}

\displaystyle p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\right)\ :=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}\mathbf{s}_{2}+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{in}}\mathbf{s}_{3}+\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)_{i}.

We define
n^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})\in\mathbb{N}
as follows:

n^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})\ :=\ \begin{cases}\left\lceil\frac{t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}-p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\right)}{q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}\right)}\right\rceil,&\text{if $q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}\right)>0$ and $p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\right)<t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}$}\\
\left\lceil\frac{p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\right)-t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}}{-q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}\right)}\right\rceil,&\text{if $q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}\right)<0$ and $p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\right)>t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}$}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
n_{\mathcal{A}}
be the maximum of
n^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})
.
Since
\mathcal{S}^{\left(\ell\right)}
has finite size, a maximum value exists.

We prove the lemma by induction on the layers of
\mathcal{A}
.
For the base case
\ell=0
, the properties hold by the definition of bipolar graphs.
For the induction step
1\leq\ell\leq L
,
we first compute the features for
v_{0}
and
v_{0}^{\prime}
.
Note that by the induction hypothesis,

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0})
,

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1})
, and

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{2})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{2})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})\ =

\displaystyle f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{out}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})+\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{2})\right)+\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0})+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{out}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1})+\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{2})\right)+\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)\ =\ \xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0}).

We can check the features of
v_{1,i}
and
v_{2,i}
similarly.

For the features of
v_{1}
,
by the induction hypothesis, it holds that

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0})
and

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1})
.
For
1\leq i\leq n_{\mathcal{A}}
and
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n_{1}
,

\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1,i})=\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1,i^{\prime}})
.
For
1\leq i\leq{d^{\left(\ell\right)}}
,
we can rewrite
\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})
and
\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1})
as follows:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G},i}(v_{1})\ =

\displaystyle\left(f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{in}}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{out}}\sum_{j\in{\left[n_{\mathcal{A}}\right]}}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1,j})+\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)\right)_{i}

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1}),\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})\right)+n_{\mathcal{A}}\cdot q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1,1})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(p+n_{\mathcal{A}}q\right)

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},i}(v^{\prime}_{1})\ =

\displaystyle\left(f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1})+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{in}}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0})+\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\text{out}}\sum_{j\in{\left[n_{1}\right]}}\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1,j})+\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}\right)\right)_{i}

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1}),\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{0})\right)+n_{1}\cdot q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}_{1,1})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(p+n_{1}q\right)

where
q:=q^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1,1})\right)
and
p:=p^{\left(\ell\right)}_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1}),\xi^{\left(\ell-1\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{0})\right)
.
We consider the following cases of
q
and
p
.
Note that
f^{\left(\ell\right)}
is eventually constant with the left threshold
t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}
and the right threshold
t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}
.

•

If
q=0
,
then
\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G},i}(v_{1})=f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(p\right)=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},i}(v^{\prime}_{1})
.

•

Suppose that
q>0
.

–

If
p\geq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}
, then

p+n_{\mathcal{A}}q\geq p\geq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}
and

p+n_{1}q\geq p\geq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}
.

–

If
p<t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}
,
by the definition of
n_{\mathcal{A}}
,

n_{1}\geq n_{\mathcal{A}}\geq\left\lceil\left(t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}-p\right)/q\right\rceil
,
which implies that
p+n_{1}q\geq p+n_{\mathcal{A}}q\geq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}
.

Hence, for both cases,

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G},i}(v_{1})=f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{right}}\right)=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},i}(v^{\prime}_{1})
.

•

Suppose that
q<0
.

–

If
p\leq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}
, then

p+n_{\mathcal{A}}q\leq p\leq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}
and

p+n_{1}q\leq p\leq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}
.

–

If
p>t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}
,
by the definition of
n_{\mathcal{A}}
,

n_{1}\geq n_{\mathcal{A}}\geq\left\lceil\left(p-t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}\right)/-q\right\rceil
,
which implies that
p+n_{1}q\leq p+n_{\mathcal{A}}q\leq t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}
.

Hence, for both cases,

\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G},i}(v_{1})=f^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(t^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathsf{left}}\right)=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},i}(v^{\prime}_{1})
.

Thus, it holds that
\xi^{\left(\ell,i\right)}_{\mathcal{G}}(v_{1})=\xi^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},i}(v^{\prime}_{1})
.

The feature of
v_{2}
can be treated analogously.
This completes the proof of the lemma.

We are now ready to show that there is a
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula that is not captured
by a
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
, which is the main claim of Lemma
6.5
:

Proof 6.9
.

Consider the following
2
-
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
formula:

\Psi(x)\ :=\ \left({\#_{z,y}\left[E(x,z)\land E(z,y)\land U_{1}(y)\right]}={\#_{z,y}\left[E(x,z)\land E(z,y)\land U_{2}(y)\right]}\right)

Let
\mathcal{G}
be the
(n_{1},n_{2})
-bipolar graph.
It is routine to check that
\mathcal{G}\models\Psi(v_{0})

if and only if
n_{1}=n_{2}
.

For every
2
-
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
,
let
n_{\mathcal{A}}
be the constant defined in Lemma
6.7
.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be the
(n_{\mathcal{A}},n_{\mathcal{A}})
-bipolar graph and

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be the
(n_{\mathcal{A}},n_{\mathcal{A}}+1)
-bipolar graph.
By Lemma
6.7
,
\xi^{L}_{\mathcal{G},1}(v_{0})=\xi^{L}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},1}(v^{\prime}_{0})
,
which implies that
\left\langle\mathcal{G},v_{0}\right\rangle
and
\left\langle\mathcal{G}^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{0}\right\rangle
are indistinguishable by
\mathcal{A}
.
On the other hand,
\mathcal{G}\models\Psi(v_{0})
but
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\not\models\Psi(v^{\prime}_{0})
,
they are distinguishable by
\Psi(x)
.
Therefore
\mathcal{A}
and
\Psi(x)
are not equivalent.

We can now prove the inclusion in Lemma

6.5
. We know that
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s are equivalent in expressiveness to
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
.
So it suffices to show that
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
is subsumed in expressiveness by
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
.
But note that each Presburger quantifier is also a two-hop Presburger quantifier with no two-hop terms.
Then it is obvious that
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
is subsumed in expressiveness by
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
.

The following results are direct consequences of the lemma above and the logical characterization in the prior section:

Corollary 6.10
.

\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{M2P^{2}}
is strictly more expressive than
\mathcal{L}{\text{-}}\mathsf{MP^{2}}
.

Corollary 6.11
.

\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s are strictly more expressive than
\mathcal{L}\mathcal{C}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s.

6.2
Undecidability of the satisfiability problem for GNNs with unbounded activations

In Section
4.2
,
we show that the satisfiability problem for
\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
is undecidable.
Since
\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
subsumes
\mathsf{TrReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
, the satisfiability problem of
\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
if also undecidable.
However, recall that for GNNs with
\mathsf{TrReLU}
activation,
the satisfiability problem is decidable when we consider
local
subclasses of GNNs with
\mathsf{TrReLU}
.
In contrast, we will prove that the satisfiability problems remain
undecidable
even for the
local
subclass of GNNs with
\mathsf{ReLU}
activation.
For
\mathsf{TrReLU}
activation we do not know what happens when we do not restrict to local aggregation, but restrict to outgoing-only aggregation on directed graphs. But for the
\mathsf{ReLU}
case, we are able to show undecidability of satisfiability in the
\mathsf{ReLU}
case even with outgoing-only aggregation. That is, the two main results of this subsection are:

Theorem 6.12
.

The satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{O}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s is undecidable.

Theorem 6.13
.

The satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{L}\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
s is undecidable.

For both results, we will reduce from Hilbert’s tenth problem to the satisfiability problem for both local and outgoing-only GNNs with
\mathsf{ReLU}
activations.
Recall that the solvability (over
\mathbb{N}
) of simple equation systems,
as defined in Definition
4.24
, is undecidable
[
16
]
. Equations take one of the forms:

\upsilon_{t_{1}}=1
,

\upsilon_{t_{1}}=\upsilon_{t_{2}}+\upsilon_{t_{3}}
,
or
\upsilon_{t_{1}}=\upsilon_{t_{2}}\cdot\upsilon_{t_{3}}
.
Encoding the solvability of the first two types of equations is straightforward.

Before presenting the encoding of simple equation systems,
we first show that for every quantifier-free Presburger formula
\varphi(\mathbf{x})
,
there exists a “
\varphi
-test GNN fragment
\mathcal{A}
:
one that does a Boolean test to check if the features of
\mathcal{A}
satisfy
\varphi(\mathbf{x})
.
The proof idea is similar to Theorem
4.17
,
with the main difference being that the input to the Presburger formula is encoded only in the features of the GNN,
rather than in the entire input graph.
Thus, the constructed GNN does not perform any aggregation.
For each subformula, there is a corresponding feature entry that represents the subformula.
The feature value is
1
if the subformula holds and
0
otherwise.

For a quantifier-free Presburger formula
\varphi(\mathbf{x})
,
the depth of
\varphi(\mathbf{x})
,
denoted by
0pt{\varphi}
,
is defined inductively as follows:

0pt{\varphi}\ =\ \begin{cases}1,&\text{if $\varphi(\mathbf{x})$ is $\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}x_{t}\geq c$}\\
0pt{\psi}+1,&\text{If $\varphi(\mathbf{x})$ is $\neg\psi(\mathbf{x})$}\\
\max_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}0pt{\psi_{t}}+1,&\text{if $\varphi(\mathbf{x})$ is
$\bigwedge_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\psi_{t}(\mathbf{x})$ or
$\bigvee_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\psi_{t}(\mathbf{x})$}.\end{cases}

For every
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
,
we denote the evaluation of
\varphi(\mathbf{x})
on
\mathbf{a}
by
{\left\llbracket\varphi(\mathbf{a})\right\rrbracket}
.
That is, if
\varphi(\mathbf{a})
holds, then
{\left\llbracket\varphi(\mathbf{a})\right\rrbracket}=1
.
Otherwise,
{\left\llbracket\varphi(\mathbf{a})\right\rrbracket}=0
.

Recall from the preliminaries that when we have a Presburger formula
\phi(x_{1}\ldots x_{n})

with a canonical ordering of variables
x_{1}\ldots x_{n})
, and an
an
n
-dimensional vector
\vec{v}
, it makes sense to say that
\phi(\vec{v})
holds. We will often apply this where the vector
\vec{v}
is a feature vector of a GNN below.

Lemma 6.14
.

For every quantifier-free Presburger formula
\Psi(\mathbf{x})
with
n
variables,
there exist matrices
\mathbf{C}^{\left(i\right)}_{\Psi}
and vectors
\mathbf{b}^{\left(i\right)}_{\Psi}
for
1\leq i\leq 20pt{\Psi}
,
such that the following property holds:
for every
\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}

\mathcal{A}
with integer coefficients,
if there exists
\ell_{0}
satisfied that

\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}=\mathbf{C}^{\left(i\right)}_{\Psi}
;

\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}=\mathbf{b}^{\left(i\right)}_{\Psi}
;

\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}_{\text{out}}
,
\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}_{\text{in}}
, and
\mathbf{R}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}
are zero matrices for
1\leq i\leq 20pt{\Psi}
,
then it holds that for every graph
\mathcal{G}
and vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}
,

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+20pt{\Psi}\right)}_{i}(v)\ =\ \begin{cases}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}_{i}(v),&\text{for $1\leq i\leq n$}\\
{\left\llbracket\Psi\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)\right\rrbracket},&\text{for $i=n+1$},\end{cases}

where
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)
is the
\ell_{0}^{th}
feature of the vertex
v
.

Proof 6.15
.

Let
L
be the number of subformulas of
\Psi(\mathbf{x})
and

\left\{\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})\right\}_{i\in{\left[L\right]}}
be an enumeration of subformulas of
\Psi(\mathbf{x})
satisfying that
\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{x})
is
\Psi(\mathbf{x})
.
For
1\leq i\leq L
, let
h_{i}:=0pt{\varphi_{i}}
.

For the first layer,
\mathbf{C}^{\left(1\right)}_{\Psi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n\times(n+L)}
and
\mathbf{b}^{\left(1\right)}_{\Psi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n+L}
.
For
2\leq\ell\leq 20pt{\ell}
,

\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{(n+L)\times(n+L)}
and
\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n+L}
.
We define the coefficients in
\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}
and
\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}
row by row as follows:
firstly, for
1\leq i\leq n
, for
1\leq\ell\leq 20pt{\Psi}
,

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{i,i}=1
.

Next, for
1\leq i\leq L
, for
2h_{i}<\ell\leq 20pt{\Psi}
,

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+i}=1
.
The definition of layers
2h_{i}
and
2h_{i}-1
depends on the formula
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
.

•

Suppose that
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}x_{t}\geq c
.
Let

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=1
,

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+i}=-1
,

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}-1\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=c
, and,
for
1\leq t\leq n
,
\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}-1\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+t}=-a_{t}
.

•

Suppose that
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\neg\varphi_{j}(\mathbf{x})
.
Let

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=1
and

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+j}=-1
.

•

Suppose that
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\bigwedge_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\varphi_{j_{t}}(\mathbf{x})
.
Let

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=1
,

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+i}=-1
,

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}-1\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=k
, and,
for
1\leq t\leq k
,
\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}-1\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+j_{t}}=-1
.

•

Suppose that
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\bigvee_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\varphi_{j_{t}}(\mathbf{x})
.
Let

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=1
,

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+i}=-1
,

\left(\mathbf{b}^{\left(2h_{i}-1\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i}=1
, and,
for
1\leq t\leq k
,
\left(\mathbf{C}^{\left(2h_{i}-1\right)}_{\Psi}\right)_{n+i,n+j_{t}}=-1
. Note that since
\varphi_{j_{t}}(x)
is a strict subformula of
\varphi_{i}(x)
,
j_{t}\neq i
, so these case definitions cannot clash.

Finally, all other entries of
\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}
and
\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell\right)}_{\Psi}
are 0.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\mathsf{ReLU}\mathsf{{\text{-}}GNN}
with integer coefficients satisfying that,

\mathbf{C}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}=\mathbf{C}^{\left(i\right)}_{\Psi}
;

\mathbf{b}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}=\mathbf{b}^{\left(i\right)}_{\Psi}
;

\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}_{\text{out}}
,
\mathbf{A}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}_{\text{in}}
, and
\mathbf{R}^{\left(\ell_{0}+i\right)}
are zero matrices for
1\leq i\leq 20pt{\Psi}
,
For every graph
\mathcal{G}
and vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}
,
we first observe that for
1\leq i\leq n
,

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+20pt{\Psi}\right)}_{i}(v)\ =\ \xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+20pt{\Psi}-1\right)}_{i}(v)\ =\ \cdots\ =\ \xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}_{i}(v),

and, for
1\leq i\leq L
,

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+20pt{\Psi}\right)}_{n+i}(v)\ =\ \xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+20pt{\Psi}-1\right)}_{n+i}(v)\ =\ \cdots\ =\ \xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v).

Next, we make a claim:
for
1\leq i\leq L
,
if
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds, then
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}(v)=1
.
Otherwise,
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}(v)=0
.
The lemma will follow the claim trivially.

We prove the claim by induction on subformulas.

•

If
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}x_{t}\geq c
, then

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)\ =\ \mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\mathsf{ReLU}\left(c-\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2\right)}_{t}(v)\right)\right).

If
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds,
then
c-\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}_{t}(v)\leq 0
.
Since
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2\right)}_{t}(v)=\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}_{t}(v)
,
it holds that
c-\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2\right)}_{t}(v)\leq 0
,
which implies that
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=1
.
On the other hand, because all coefficients in
\mathcal{A}
are integers,

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)
is an integer vector.
If
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold,
then
c-\sum_{t\in{\left[n\right]}}a_{t}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}_{t}(v)\geq 1
,
which implies that

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=0
.

•

If
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\neg\varphi_{j}(\mathbf{x})
, then

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)\ =\ \mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-1\right)}_{n+j}(v)\right).

If
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds,
then
\varphi_{j}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold.
Since
h_{i}=h_{j}+1
,
\ell_{0}+2h_{j}\leq\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-1
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-1\right)}_{n+j}(v)=0
.
Thus,
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=1
.
Otherwise, if
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold,
then
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-1\right)}_{n+j}(v)=1
and
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=0
.

•

If
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\bigwedge_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\varphi_{j_{t}}(\mathbf{x})
, then

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)\ =\ \mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\mathsf{ReLU}\left(k-\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2\right)}_{n+j_{t}}(v)\right)\right).

If
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds,
then, for
1\leq t\leq k
,
\varphi_{j_{t}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds.
Since
h_{j_{t}}<h_{i}
,
\ell_{0}+2h_{j_{t}}\leq\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\varphi_{j_{t}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)=1
.
Thus,
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=1
.
Otherwise, if
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold,
then there exists some
1\leq t\leq k

such that
\varphi_{j_{t}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold and

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=0
.

•

If
\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
is
\bigvee_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\varphi_{j_{t}}(\mathbf{x})
, then

\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)\ =\ \mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\mathsf{ReLU}\left(1-\sum_{t\in{\left[k\right]}}\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2\right)}_{n+j_{t}}(v)\right)\right).

If
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds,
then there exists some
1\leq t\leq k
such that
\varphi_{j_{t}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
holds.
Since
h_{j_{t}}<h_{i}
,
\ell_{0}+2h_{j_{t}}\leq\ell_{0}+2h_{i}-2
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\varphi_{j_{t}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)=1
.
Thus,
\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}+2h_{i}\right)}_{n+i}(v)=1
.
Otherwise, if
\varphi_{i}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold,
then,
1\leq t\leq k
,
\varphi_{j_{t}}\left(\xi^{\left(\ell_{0}\right)}(v)\right)
doesn’t hold and
